   if one appears anyway we still flatten it conservatively. *)
let flatten_namespaces (file_name : string) (prog : program)
  : (string list * top_decl list) list =
  let buckets : (string list, top_decl list ref) Hashtbl.t = Hashtbl.create 4 in
  let order : string list list ref = ref [] in
  let bucket_for path =
    match Hashtbl.find_opt buckets path with
    | Some r -> r
    | None ->
        let r = ref [] in
        Hashtbl.add buckets path r;
        order := path :: !order;
        r
  in
  let rec visit path decls =
    List.iter (fun d ->
      match d with
      | TopNamespace (sub, inner) -> visit sub inner
      | _ ->
          let r = bucket_for path in
          r := d :: !r) decls
  in
  visit [file_name] prog;
  List.rev_map (fun path ->
    let r = Hashtbl.find buckets path in
    (path, List.rev !r)) !order

let summarize (module_path : string list) (prog : top_decl list) : module_summary =
  let m n = mangle_for_module module_path n in
  let types   = ref [] in
  let records = ref [] in
  let fns     = ref [] in
  let ctors   = ref [] in
  let aliases = ref [] in
  let externs = ref [] in
  List.iter (fun decl ->
    match decl with
    | TopType td ->
        types := (td.type_name, m td.type_name) :: !types;
        List.iter (fun v ->
          ctors := (v.ctor_name, m v.ctor_name) :: !ctors) td.variants
    | TopRecord rd ->
        records := (rd.rec_name, m rd.rec_name) :: !records
    | TopFunc f ->
        fns := (f.name, m f.name) :: !fns
    | TopExtern e ->
        externs := e.ext_name :: !externs
    | TopAlias a ->
        aliases := (a.alias_name, m a.alias_name) :: !aliases
    | TopConst c ->
        (* A const resolves like a (0-arg) function name, and its mangled
           name is recorded so uses get rewritten to calls. *)
        fns := (c.const_name, m c.const_name) :: !fns;
        Hashtbl.replace const_set (m c.const_name) ()
    | TopTest _ -> ()  (* test blocks don't introduce namespace-level names *)
    | TopUse _ -> ()
    | TopNamespace _ -> ()  (* flattened away by flatten_namespaces *)
  ) prog;
  { mod_path     = module_path;
    type_names   = !types;
    record_names = !records;
    fn_names     = !fns;
    ctor_names   = !ctors;
    alias_names  = !aliases;
    extern_names = !externs;
    re_exports   = [] }

(* Second pass: fill in re_exports for every namespace from its
   `pub use foo::{bar};` decls. Has to come after every namespace's
   bare summary is built, because a re-export needs to look the
   item up in the source namespace's summary. *)
let fill_re_exports
    (summaries : module_summary list)
    (per_ns : (string list * top_decl list) list) : unit =
  let find_summary p =
    try List.find (fun s -> s.mod_path = p) summaries
    with Not_found ->
      raise (Resolve_error
        (Printf.sprintf "namespace %S not found (referenced by `pub use`)"
           (path_to_string p)))
  in
  let lookup_in (s : module_summary) (item : string) : string option =
    match List.assoc_opt item s.type_names with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    match List.assoc_opt item s.record_names with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    match List.assoc_opt item s.fn_names with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    match List.assoc_opt item s.ctor_names with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    match List.assoc_opt item s.alias_names with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    match List.assoc_opt item s.re_exports with Some m -> Some m | None ->
    if List.mem item s.extern_names then Some item else None
  in
  List.iter (fun (ns_path, prog) ->
    let m_summary = find_summary ns_path in
    List.iter (fun decl ->
      match decl with
      | TopUse u when u.use_pub ->
          let src = find_summary u.use_module in
          List.iter (fun item ->
            match lookup_in src item with
            | Some target ->
                m_summary.re_exports <- (item, target) :: m_summary.re_exports
            | None ->
                raise (Resolve_error
                  (Printf.sprintf
                     "pub use %s::%s — %S is not declared in namespace %S"
                     (path_to_string u.use_module) item item
                     (path_to_string u.use_module)))) u.use_items
      | _ -> ()) prog) per_ns

(* For a namespace M, build the resolution table that maps bare names
   visible in M's source to their target form (mangled or unchanged). *)
let build_resolution_map
    (m_summary : module_summary)
    (uses : use_decl list)
    (all_summaries : module_summary list)
  : (string * string) list =
  let own =
    m_summary.type_names
    @ m_summary.record_names
    @ m_summary.fn_names
    @ m_summary.ctor_names
    @ m_summary.alias_names
    @ List.map (fun e -> (e, e)) m_summary.extern_names
  in
  let imports =
    List.concat_map (fun u ->
      let other =
        try List.find (fun s -> s.mod_path = u.use_module) all_summaries
        with Not_found ->
          raise (Resolve_error
            (Printf.sprintf "namespace %S not found (referenced by `use`)"
               (path_to_string u.use_module)))
      in
      List.map (fun item ->
        let lookup name lst = List.assoc_opt name lst in
        match lookup item other.type_names with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
        match lookup item other.record_names with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
        match lookup item other.fn_names with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
        match lookup item other.ctor_names with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
        match lookup item other.alias_names with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
        match lookup item other.re_exports with
        | Some m -> (item, m)
        | None ->
          if List.mem item other.extern_names then (item, item)
          else raise (Resolve_error
            (Printf.sprintf
               "use %s::%s — %S is not declared in namespace %S"
               (path_to_string u.use_module) item item
               (path_to_string u.use_module)))) u.use_items)
      uses
  in
  let bare_own = List.map fst own in
  List.iter (fun (n, _) ->
    if List.mem n bare_own then
      raise (Resolve_error
        (Printf.sprintf
           "imported name %S clashes with a local declaration in namespace %S"
           n (path_to_string m_summary.mod_path)))) imports;
  let rec check_dups = function
    | [] -> ()
    | (x, _) :: rest ->
        if List.exists (fun (y, _) -> y = x) rest then
          raise (Resolve_error
            (Printf.sprintf "duplicate import of %S in namespace %S"
               x (path_to_string m_summary.mod_path)));
        check_dups rest
  in
  check_dups imports;
  own @ imports

let resolve_name
    (map : (string * string) list) (locals : string list) (name : string)
  : string =
  if String.contains name ':' then
    (* Qualified `mod::item` — mangle to `mod__item`, the same form a
       module's own decls take. Absolute, so the local resolution map is
       not consulted. *)
    String.split_on_char ':' name
    |> List.filter (fun s -> s <> "")
    |> String.concat "__"
  else if List.mem name locals then name
  else if is_builtin name then name
  else
    match List.assoc_opt name map with
    | Some renamed -> renamed
    | None -> name

let rec resolve_ty
    (map : (string * string) list) (locals : string list) (t : ty) : ty =
  match t with
  | TyInt | TyBool -> t
  | TyVar n -> TyVar n
  | TyApp (n, args) ->
      let args = List.map (resolve_ty map locals) args in
      TyApp (resolve_name map locals n, args)
  | TyFun (args, ret) ->
      TyFun (List.map (resolve_ty map locals) args,
             resolve_ty map locals ret)
  | TyPtr inner -> TyPtr (resolve_ty map locals inner)
  | TyTuple ts -> TyTuple (List.map (resolve_ty map locals) ts)
  | TyMeta _ -> t

let rec resolve_expr
    (map : (string * string) list) (locals : string list) (e : expr) : expr =
  let r = resolve_expr map locals in
  let rt = resolve_ty map locals in
  match e with
  | EInt _ | EFloat _ | EBool _ | EStringLit _ -> e
  | EVar x ->
      let resolved = resolve_name map locals x in
      (* A use of a const name becomes a call to its lowered 0-arg fn.
         Locals shadow consts and are left as plain variable refs. *)
      if not (List.mem x locals) && Hashtbl.mem const_set resolved
      then ECall (EVar resolved, [])
      else EVar resolved
  | EBinop (op, a, b) -> EBinop (op, r a, r b)
  | EUnop  (op, a)    -> EUnop  (op, r a)
  | ECall (callee, args) -> ECall (r callee, List.map r args)
  | EFun (params, ret, body) ->
      let params' = List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, rt t)) params in
      let new_locals = List.map fst params @ locals in
      EFun (params', rt ret, resolve_expr map new_locals body)
  | EClosure (rg, params, ret, body) ->
      let params' = List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, rt t)) params in
      let new_locals = List.map fst params @ locals in
      EClosure (r rg, params', rt ret, resolve_expr map new_locals body)
  | ECtor (c, args) ->
      ECtor (resolve_name map locals c, List.map r args)
  | ERecord (name, elems) ->
      let elems = List.map (function
        | RAssign (f, v) -> RAssign (f, r v)
        | RSpread b -> RSpread (r b)) elems
      in
      ERecord (resolve_name map locals name, elems)
  | EField (e, f) -> EField (r e, f)
  | EIf (c, t, el) -> EIf (r c, r t, r el)
  | ELet (x, m, asc, v, body) ->
      let v' = r v in
      let asc' = Option.map rt asc in
      let new_locals = if x = "_" then locals else x :: locals in
      let body' = resolve_expr map new_locals body in
      ELet (x, m, asc', v', body')
  | EArena (x, v, body) ->
      let v' = r v in
      let new_locals = x :: locals in
      let body' = resolve_expr map new_locals body in
      EArena (x, v', body')
  | EAssign (x, v) -> EAssign (resolve_name map locals x, r v)
  | EAssignField (p, f, v) -> EAssignField (r p, f, r v)
  | EWhile (c, b) -> EWhile (r c, r b)
  | EBreak | EContinue -> e
  | EReturn v -> EReturn (r v)
  | EMatch (s, arms) ->
      let s' = r s in
      let rec resolve_pat p =
        match p with
        | PCtor (c, vs) -> PCtor (resolve_name map locals c, vs)
        | POr pats -> POr (List.map resolve_pat pats)
        | PTuple ps -> PTuple (List.map resolve_pat ps)
        | PInt _ | PBool _ | PStr _ | PBind _ -> p
      in
      let rec pattern_locals p =
        match p with
        | PCtor (_, vs) -> List.filter (fun v -> v <> "_") vs
        | POr _ -> []
        | PTuple ps -> List.concat_map pattern_locals ps
        | PInt _ | PBool _ | PStr _ -> []
        | PBind "_" -> []
        | PBind x -> [x]
      in
      let arms' = List.map (fun (p, guard, body) ->
        let p' = resolve_pat p in
        let new_locals = pattern_locals p @ locals in
        let guard' = Option.map (resolve_expr map new_locals) guard in
        (p', guard', resolve_expr map new_locals body)) arms
      in
      EMatch (s', arms')
  | EHandle (rg, n, v) -> EHandle (r rg, r n, r v)
  | EHandleLit (rg, elems) -> EHandleLit (r rg, List.map r elems)
  | ERegion n -> ERegion (r n)
  | EStackRegion n -> EStackRegion (r n)
  | EAlignedRegion (n, a) -> EAlignedRegion (r n, r a)
  | EIndex (a, i) -> EIndex (r a, r i)
  | EAssignIdx (a, i, v) -> EAssignIdx (r a, r i, r v)
  | ELen e -> ELen (r e)
  | ESlice (a, lo, hi) -> ESlice (r a, r lo, r hi)
  | EToInt e -> EToInt (r e)
  | EToByte e -> EToByte (r e)
  | EToFloat e -> EToFloat (r e)
  | ECast (t, e) -> ECast (t, r e)
  | ECAlloc (t, n) -> ECAlloc (rt t, r n)
  | ECFree p -> ECFree (r p)
  | ENullPtr t -> ENullPtr (rt t)
  | EIsNull p -> EIsNull (r p)
  | EHandleData a -> EHandleData (r a)
  | EPtrCast (t, e) -> EPtrCast (rt t, r e)
  | EDeref p -> EDeref (r p)
  | ETryAt (a, i) -> ETryAt (r a, r i)
  | EDrop e -> EDrop (r e)
  | EReset e -> EReset (r e)
  | EAwait e -> EAwait (r e)
  | EAwaitAll branches -> EAwaitAll (List.map r branches)
  | EAwaitAllDyn e -> EAwaitAllDyn (r e)
  | ESpawn e -> ESpawn (r e)
  | EForStream (x, src, body) ->
      let src' = r src in
      let new_locals = x :: locals in
      let body' = resolve_expr map new_locals body in
      EForStream (x, src', body')
  | ETuple es -> ETuple (List.map r es)
  | ETupleIdx (e, i) -> ETupleIdx (r e, i)
  | ELetTuple (vs, v, body) ->
      let v' = r v in
      let new_locals =
        List.fold_left (fun acc n -> if n = "_" then acc else n :: acc)
          locals vs
      in
      let body' = resolve_expr map new_locals body in
      ELetTuple (vs, v', body')
  | EPrint (nl, e) -> EPrint (nl, r e)

let resolve_decl
    (map : (string * string) list) (mod_path : string list) (decl : top_decl)
  : top_decl option =
  let m_name n = mangle_for_module mod_path n in
  match decl with
  | TopUse _ -> None
  | TopNamespace _ -> None  (* flattened away upstream *)
  | TopAlias a ->
      Some (TopAlias {
        alias_name = m_name a.alias_name;
        alias_ty   = resolve_ty map [] a.alias_ty;
      })
  | TopConst c ->
      (* Lower a const to a 0-argument function. Uses of the const name
         are rewritten to calls in resolve_expr. *)
      Some (TopFunc {
        name        = m_name c.const_name;
        type_params = [];
        params      = [];
        return_ty   = resolve_ty map [] c.const_ty;
        body        = resolve_expr map [] c.const_value;
      })
  | TopType td ->
      let type_params = td.type_params in
      let locals = type_params in
      let variants = List.map (fun v ->
        { ctor_name = m_name v.ctor_name;
          arg_tys = List.map (resolve_ty map locals) v.arg_tys })
        td.variants
      in
      Some (TopType {
        type_name = m_name td.type_name;
        type_params; variants;
        is_linear = td.is_linear;
      })
  | TopRecord rd ->
      let type_params = rd.rec_type_params in
      let locals = type_params in
      let rec_fields = List.map (fun (fn, ft) ->
        (fn, resolve_ty map locals ft)) rd.rec_fields
      in
      Some (TopRecord {
        rec_name = m_name rd.rec_name;
        rec_type_params = type_params;
        rec_fields;
        rec_is_linear = rd.rec_is_linear;
      })
  | TopFunc f ->
      let type_params = f.type_params in
      let locals = type_params in
      let params = List.map (fun (pn, pt) ->
        (pn, resolve_ty map locals pt)) f.params
      in
      let return_ty = resolve_ty map locals f.return_ty in
      let param_locals = List.map fst params in
      let body_locals = param_locals @ type_params in
      let body = resolve_expr map body_locals f.body in
      Some (TopFunc {
        name = m_name f.name;
        type_params; params; return_ty; body;
      })
  | TopExtern e ->
      let params = List.map (fun (pn, pt) ->
        (pn, resolve_ty map [] pt)) e.ext_params
      in
      let return_ty = resolve_ty map [] e.ext_return_ty in
      Some (TopExtern {
        ext_name = e.ext_name;
        ext_params = params;
        ext_return_ty = return_ty;
      })
  | TopTest td ->
      (* Test bodies are normal expressions resolved against the
         module's import map. Test name kept verbatim. *)
      let body = resolve_expr map [] td.test_body in
      Some (TopTest { test_name = td.test_name; test_body = body })

(* Expand type aliases transitively, with cycle detection. *)
let rec expand_alias_ty
    (aliases : (string * ty) list) (visited : string list) (t : ty) : ty =
  match t with
  | TyInt | TyBool -> t
  | TyVar _ -> t
  | TyMeta _ -> t
  | TyApp (n, args) ->
      let args = List.map (expand_alias_ty aliases visited) args in
      (match List.assoc_opt n aliases with
       | Some target ->
           if List.mem n visited then
             raise (Resolve_error
               (Printf.sprintf "cyclic type alias: %s"
                  (String.concat " -> " (List.rev (n :: visited)))));
           if args <> [] then
             raise (Resolve_error
               (Printf.sprintf
                  "type alias %S cannot take type arguments" n));
           expand_alias_ty aliases (n :: visited) target
       | None -> TyApp (n, args))
  | TyFun (args, ret) ->
      TyFun (List.map (expand_alias_ty aliases visited) args,
             expand_alias_ty aliases visited ret)
  | TyPtr inner -> TyPtr (expand_alias_ty aliases visited inner)
  | TyTuple ts -> TyTuple (List.map (expand_alias_ty aliases visited) ts)

let expand_in_expr (aliases : (string * ty) list) (e : expr) : expr =
  let xt t = expand_alias_ty aliases [] t in
  let rec ex e =
    match e with
    | EInt _ | EFloat _ | EBool _ | EStringLit _ | EVar _
    | EBreak | EContinue -> e
    | EBinop (op, a, b) -> EBinop (op, ex a, ex b)
    | EUnop (op, a) -> EUnop (op, ex a)
    | ECall (c, args) -> ECall (ex c, List.map ex args)
    | EFun (params, ret, body) ->
        EFun (List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, xt t)) params, xt ret, ex body)
    | EClosure (rg, params, ret, body) ->
        EClosure (ex rg, List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, xt t)) params,
                  xt ret, ex body)
    | ECtor (c, args) -> ECtor (c, List.map ex args)
    | ERecord (n, elems) ->
        ERecord (n, List.map (function
          | RAssign (f, v) -> RAssign (f, ex v)
          | RSpread b -> RSpread (ex b)) elems)
    | EField (e, f) -> EField (ex e, f)
    | EIf (c, t, e) -> EIf (ex c, ex t, ex e)
    | ELet (x, m, asc, v, b) -> ELet (x, m, Option.map xt asc, ex v, ex b)
    | EArena (x, v, b) -> EArena (x, ex v, ex b)
    | EAssign (x, v) -> EAssign (x, ex v)
    | EAssignField (p, f, v) -> EAssignField (ex p, f, ex v)
    | EWhile (c, b) -> EWhile (ex c, ex b)
    | EReturn v -> EReturn (ex v)
    | EMatch (s, arms) ->
        EMatch (ex s, List.map (fun (p, g, b) ->
          (p, Option.map ex g, ex b)) arms)
    | EHandle (r, n, v) -> EHandle (ex r, ex n, ex v)
    | EHandleLit (r, elems) -> EHandleLit (ex r, List.map ex elems)
    | ERegion n -> ERegion (ex n)
    | EStackRegion n -> EStackRegion (ex n)
    | EAlignedRegion (n, a) -> EAlignedRegion (ex n, ex a)
    | EIndex (a, i) -> EIndex (ex a, ex i)
    | EAssignIdx (a, i, v) -> EAssignIdx (ex a, ex i, ex v)
    | ELen e -> ELen (ex e)
    | ESlice (a, lo, hi) -> ESlice (ex a, ex lo, ex hi)
    | EToInt e -> EToInt (ex e)
    | EToByte e -> EToByte (ex e)
    | EToFloat e -> EToFloat (ex e)
    | ECast (t, e) -> ECast (t, ex e)
    | ECAlloc (t, n) -> ECAlloc (xt t, ex n)
    | ECFree p -> ECFree (ex p)
    | ENullPtr t -> ENullPtr (xt t)
    | EIsNull p -> EIsNull (ex p)
    | EHandleData a -> EHandleData (ex a)
    | EPtrCast (t, e) -> EPtrCast (xt t, ex e)
    | EDeref p -> EDeref (ex p)
    | ETryAt (a, i) -> ETryAt (ex a, ex i)
    | EDrop e -> EDrop (ex e)
    | EReset e -> EReset (ex e)
    | EAwait e -> EAwait (ex e)
    | EAwaitAll branches -> EAwaitAll (List.map ex branches)
    | EAwaitAllDyn e -> EAwaitAllDyn (ex e)
    | ESpawn e -> ESpawn (ex e)
    | EForStream (x, s, b) -> EForStream (x, ex s, ex b)
    | ETuple es -> ETuple (List.map ex es)
    | ETupleIdx (e, i) -> ETupleIdx (ex e, i)
    | ELetTuple (vs, v, b) -> ELetTuple (vs, ex v, ex b)
    | EPrint (nl, e) -> EPrint (nl, ex e)
  in
  ex e

let expand_in_decl (aliases : (string * ty) list) (d : top_decl) : top_decl =
  let xt t = expand_alias_ty aliases [] t in
  match d with
  | TopType td ->
      TopType { td with variants =
        List.map (fun v ->
          { v with arg_tys = List.map xt v.arg_tys }) td.variants;
        is_linear = td.is_linear; }
  | TopRecord rd ->
      TopRecord { rd with rec_fields =
        List.map (fun (f, t) -> (f, xt t)) rd.rec_fields;
        rec_is_linear = rd.rec_is_linear; }
  | TopFunc f ->
      TopFunc { f with
        params = List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, xt t)) f.params;
        return_ty = xt f.return_ty;
        body = expand_in_expr aliases f.body; }
  | TopExtern e ->
      TopExtern { e with
        ext_params = List.map (fun (n, t) -> (n, xt t)) e.ext_params;
        ext_return_ty = xt e.ext_return_ty; }
  | TopTest td -> TopTest { td with test_body = expand_in_expr aliases td.test_body }
  | TopUse _ | TopAlias _ | TopConst _ -> d
  | TopNamespace _ -> d

(* Top-level entry: take an ordered list of (file_name, parsed program),
   flatten namespace blocks into per-namespace decl lists, resolve all
   references, return one merged program ready for the type checker.
   Externs are deduplicated by name. *)
let resolve (modules : (string * program) list) : program =
  Hashtbl.clear const_set;
  (* Step 1: each file → list of (namespace_path, decls). Multiple
     files contributing to the same namespace path are merged. *)
  let merged : (string list * top_decl list) list =
    let acc : (string list, top_decl list ref) Hashtbl.t = Hashtbl.create 8 in
    let order : string list list ref = ref [] in
    List.iter (fun (fn, prog) ->
      let per_ns = flatten_namespaces fn prog in
      List.iter (fun (path, decls) ->
        match Hashtbl.find_opt acc path with
        | Some r -> r := !r @ decls
        | None ->
            Hashtbl.add acc path (ref decls);
            order := path :: !order) per_ns) modules;
    List.rev_map (fun path -> (path, !(Hashtbl.find acc path))) !order
  in
  let summaries = List.map (fun (p, decls) -> summarize p decls) merged in
  fill_re_exports summaries merged;
  let resolved_per_module = List.map (fun (path, prog) ->
    let m_summary = List.find (fun s -> s.mod_path = path) summaries in
    let uses = List.filter_map (function
      | TopUse u -> Some u
      | _ -> None) prog
    in
    let map = build_resolution_map m_summary uses summaries in
    List.filter_map (resolve_decl map path) prog
  ) merged
  in
  let combined = List.concat resolved_per_module in
  (* Collect aliases by their mangled name, then expand all references
     to them throughout the program. *)
  let aliases =
    List.filter_map (function
      | TopAlias a -> Some (a.alias_name, a.alias_ty)
      | _ -> None) combined
  in
  let expanded = List.map (expand_in_decl aliases) combined in
  let no_aliases = List.filter (function
    | TopAlias _ -> false | _ -> true) expanded
  in
  (* Deduplicate externs by name — multiple modules can declare the
     same C function (e.g. putchar). First occurrence wins; later
     duplicates with different signatures are caught later by check. *)
  let seen_externs = Hashtbl.create 8 in
  List.filter (function
    | TopExtern e ->
        if Hashtbl.mem seen_externs e.ext_name then false
        else (Hashtbl.add seen_externs e.ext_name (); true)
    | _ -> true) no_aliases

===== END FILE: lib/resolve.ml =====

===== BEGIN FILE: lib/token.ml =====
(* Tokens. *)

type token =
  (* literals *)
  | TInt of int64
  | TFloat of float
  | TIdent of string
  | TCtorIdent of string
  | TStringLit of string   (* "..." — byte string literal *)
  (* keywords *)
  | TFn
  | TLet
  | TMut          (* mut — `let mut x = ...;` for reassignable bindings *)
  | TIf
  | TElse
  | TWhile        (* while cond { body } *)
  | TBreak        (* break; — early exit from while *)
  | TContinue     (* continue; — skip to next iteration *)
  | TFor          (* for i in lo..hi { body } — sugar for while+mut *)
  | TIn           (* in — used in `for i in lo..hi` *)
  | TReturn       (* return expr; — early exit from a function *)
  | TTrue
  | TFalse
  | TIntTy
  | TBoolTy
  | TByteTy       (* byte — 1-byte unsigned primitive *)
  | TU16Ty        (* u16 — 2-byte unsigned, for binary protocols *)
  | TU32Ty        (* u32 — 4-byte unsigned *)
  | TU64Ty        (* u64 — 8-byte unsigned *)
  | TFloatTy      (* float — IEEE 754 double-precision *)
  | TToFloat      (* to_float(n) — int → float *)
  | TType
  | TMatch
  | TExtern
  | TStruct
  | TEnum
  | TLinear        (* linear — marks a struct/enum as move-only with user drop *)
  | TDrop          (* drop(x) — consume and run the type's destructor *)
  | TUse           (* use mod::item; — selective import *)
  | TConst         (* const NAME: T = expr; — named compile-time value *)
  | TLen          (* len — array length *)
  | TSlice        (* slice(a, lo, hi) — sub-handle into the same region *)
  | TToInt        (* to_int(b) — widen byte to int *)
  | TToByte       (* to_byte(n) — truncate int to byte *)
  | TCAlloc       (* c_alloc[T](n) — malloc n*sizeof(T), returns *T *)
  | TCFree        (* c_free(p) — free a raw pointer *)
  | TNullPtr      (* null_ptr[T]() — typed NULL *)
  | TIsNull       (* is_null(p) — NULL check *)
  | TAsPtr    (* as_ptr(a) — *T pointing at the bytes of Handle[T] *)
  | TPtrCast      (* ptr_cast[T](e) — reinterpret a raw pointer/address as *T *)
  | TReset        (* reset(r) — rewind a region, invalidating its refs *)
  | TTryAt        (* try_at(a, i) — defensive read, returns Option[T] *)
  | TRegion       (* region(N) — heap arena, malloc'd block *)
  | TStackRegion  (* stack_region(N) — N literal, block on stack *)
  | TAlignedRegion (* aligned_region(N, A) — heap, A-byte aligned *)
  | TAwait        (* await — Stage 3 async suspension point *)
  | TSpawn        (* spawn f(...) — Stage 3 fire-and-forget task *)
  | TYield        (* yield — Stage 3 cooperative scheduling point *)
  | TUnderscore   (* `_` as a standalone token (wildcard pattern) *)
  | TQuestion     (* postfix `?` — Result early-return *)
  | TPub          (* `pub use foo::{a};` — re-export *)
  | TTest         (* `test "name" { body }` — test block, --test mode *)
  | TNamespace    (* `namespace foo::bar { decls }` — nested namespace block *)
  | TPrint        (* print(expr)    — intrinsic: writev a tuple or scalar *)
  | TPrintln      (* println(expr)  — same, plus trailing '\n' *)
  | TArena        (* arena r = region(N); — scope-bound region binding *)
  | TClosure      (* closure(r, fn...) — capturing lambda, env in region r *)
  | TRef          (* ref(r, v) — allocate cell(s) in region r, return Handle[T] *)
  (* punctuation *)
  | TLParen
  | TRParen
  | TLBrace
  | TRBrace
  | TLBracket     (* [ *)
  | TRBracket     (* ] *)
  | TComma
  | TColon
  | TColonCol     (* :: — module path separator *)
  | TSemi
  | TEq
  | TArrow        (* -> *)
  | TFatArrow     (* => *)
  | TPipe         (* |  *)
  | TPipeArrow    (* |> — pipeline: x |> f means f(x) *)
  | TAmp          (* &  bitwise AND *)
  | TCaret        (* ^  bitwise XOR *)
  | TTilde        (* ~  bitwise NOT *)
  | TShl          (* << *)
  | TShr          (* >> *)
  | TPlus         (* +  *)
  | TMinus        (* -  *)
  | TStar         (* *  *)
  | TSlash        (* /  *)
  | TPercent      (* %  *)
  | TEqEq         (* == *)
  | TNeq          (* != *)
  | TLt           (* <  *)
  | TGt           (* >  *)
  | TLe           (* <= *)
  | TGe           (* >= *)
  | TAndAnd       (* && *)
  | TOrOr         (* || *)
  | TBang         (* !  *)
  | TDot          (* .  *)
  | TDotDot       (* .. *)
  | TColonEq      (* := — only for array index assignment a[i] := v *)
  | TEOF

let show = function
  | TInt n        -> Printf.sprintf "INT(%Ld)" n
  | TFloat f      -> Printf.sprintf "FLOAT(%g)" f
  | TIdent s      -> Printf.sprintf "IDENT(%s)" s
  | TCtorIdent s  -> Printf.sprintf "CTOR(%s)" s
  | TStringLit s  -> Printf.sprintf "STR(%S)" s
  | TFn           -> "FN"

  | TU16Ty        -> "U16_TY"
  | TU32Ty        -> "U32_TY"

  | TFloatTy      -> "FLOAT_TY"
  | TToFloat      -> "TO_FLOAT"
  | TType         -> "TYPE"
  | TMatch        -> "MATCH"
  | TExtern       -> "EXTERN"
  | TStruct       -> "STRUCT"
  | TEnum         -> "ENUM"
  | TLinear       -> "LINEAR"
  | TDrop         -> "DROP"
  | TUse          -> "USE"
  | TConst        -> "CONST"
  | TLen          -> "LEN"
  | TSlice        -> "SLICE"
  | TToInt        -> "TO_INT"
  | TToByte       -> "TO_BYTE"
  | TCAlloc       -> "C_ALLOC"
  | TCFree        -> "C_FREE"
  | TNullPtr      -> "NULL_PTR"
  | TIsNull       -> "IS_NULL"
  | TAsPtr    -> "AS_PTR"
  | TPtrCast      -> "PTR_CAST"
  | TReset        -> "RESET"
  | TTryAt        -> "TRY_AT"
  | TRegion       -> "REGION"
  | TStackRegion  -> "STACK_REGION"
  | TAlignedRegion -> "ALIGNED_REGION"
  | TAwait        -> "AWAIT"
  | TSpawn        -> "SPAWN"
  | TYield        -> "YIELD"
  | TUnderscore   -> "_"
  | TQuestion     -> "?"
  | TPub          -> "PUB"
  | TTest         -> "TEST"
  | TNamespace    -> "NAMESPACE"
  | TPrint        -> "PRINT"
  | TPrintln      -> "PRINTLN"
  | TArena        -> "ARENA"
  | TClosure      -> "CLOSURE"
  | TRef          -> "REF"
  | TLParen       -> "("
  | TRParen       -> ")"
  | TLBrace       -> "{"
  | TRBrace       -> "}"
  | TLBracket     -> "["
  | TRBracket     -> "]"
  | TComma        -> ","
  | TColon        -> ":"
  | TColonCol     -> "::"
  | TSemi         -> ";"
  | TEq           -> "="
  | TArrow        -> "->"
  | TFatArrow     -> "=>"
  | TPipe         -> "|"
  | TPipeArrow    -> "|>"
  | TAmp          -> "&"
  | TCaret        -> "^"
  | TTilde        -> "~"
  | TShl          -> "<<"
  | TShr          -> ">>"
  | TPlus         -> "+"
  | TMinus        -> "-"
  | TStar         -> "*"
  | TSlash        -> "/"
  | TPercent      -> "%"
  | TEqEq         -> "=="
  | TNeq          -> "!="
  | TLt           -> "<"
  | TGt           -> ">"
  | TLe           -> "<="
  | TGe           -> ">="
  | TAndAnd       -> "&&"
  | TOrOr         -> "||"
  | TBang         -> "!"

  | TDotDot       -> ".."

  | TEOF          -> "EOF"

===== BEGIN FILE: std/fmt.orto =====
// std/fmt.orto — string formatting with `{}` placeholders. No printf
// mini-language and no type-directed magic: you convert your values to
// strings yourself (e.g. str::from_int) and pass them as `parts`. The
// formatter just walks the template and drops each part into the next
// `{}` slot, in order. `{{` is a literal `{`, `}}` a literal `}`.

use vec::{Vec, new, push, slice_of};

fn format(r: Region, template: Handle[byte], parts: Handle[Handle[byte]]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let mut out = new(r, len(template) + 8, to_byte(0));
    let n = len(template);
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut k = 0;
    while i < n {
        let c = to_int(template[i]);
        if c == 123 {                                  // '{'
            if i + 1 < n {
                let c2 = to_int(template[i + 1]);
                if c2 == 125 {                         // '}'  -> a slot
                    let p = parts[k];
                    let mut j = 0;
                    while j < len(p) {
                        out := push(out, r, p[j]);
                        j := j + 1;
                    }
                    k := k + 1;
                    i := i + 2;
                } else {
                    if c2 == 123 {                     // '{{' -> literal '{'
                        out := push(out, r, template[i]);
                        i := i + 2;
                    } else {
                        out := push(out, r, template[i]);
                        i := i + 1;
                    }
                }
            } else {
                out := push(out, r, template[i]);
                i := i + 1;
            }
        } else {
            if c == 125 && i + 1 < n && to_int(template[i + 1]) == 125 {
                out := push(out, r, template[i]);     // '}}' -> literal '}'
                i := i + 2;
            } else {
                out := push(out, r, template[i]);
                i := i + 1;
            }
        }
    }
    slice_of(out)
}

// Format and write a line to stdout. Takes NO region: the formatted
// string never escapes this call, so the function provisions its own
// scratch arena, fills it, writes, and frees it on return. The caller's
// region is for values the caller keeps — not for throwaway output.
fn line(template: Handle[byte], parts: Handle[Handle[byte]]) -> () {
    let mut total = len(template);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < len(parts) { total := total + len(parts[i]); i := i + 1; }
    arena s = region((total + 16) * 2);
    println(format(s, template, parts));
}


===== END FILE: std/fmt.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/map.orto =====
// std/map.orto — an open-addressing hash map (linear probing) with
// power-of-two capacity, so the slot index is a single bitwise AND
// (`h & (cap-1)`) instead of a modulo. Grows and rehashes at ~75% load.
// Deletion uses tombstones; a grow drops them.
//
// Behaviour is injected, not baked into types: the map stores its own
// `hash` and `eq` as closures — the explicit, visible form of a
// type-class dictionary, chosen per map. Keys and values are copied in.
// `zk`/`zv` are sample zero values used to fill backing slots.

use vec::{Vec};

// slot state: 0 = empty, 1 = used, 2 = tombstone
struct Entry[K, V] { state: int, key: K, val: V }

struct Map[K, V] {
    slots : Handle[Entry[K, V]],
    cap   : int,           // always a power of two
    count : int,           // live entries
    occ   : int,           // used + tombstones (probe-chain occupancy)
    hash  : fn(K) -> int,
    eq    : fn(K, K) -> bool,
    ze    : Entry[K, V]
}

fn pow2_at_least(n: int) -> int {
    let mut p = 4;
    while p < n { p := p << 1; }
    p
}

// Slot index from a hash. cap is a power of two, so the low bits are a
// uniform index even when h is negative (two's-complement low bits).
fn slot(h: int, cap: int) -> int { h & (cap - 1) }

fn new[K, V](r: Region, cap: int, zk: K, zv: V,
             hash: fn(K) -> int, eq: fn(K, K) -> bool) -> Map[K, V] {
    let c = pow2_at_least(cap);
    let ze = Entry { state: 0, key: zk, val: zv };
    Map { slots: ref(r, c, ze), cap: c, count: 0, occ: 0,
          hash: hash, eq: eq, ze: ze }
}

// Index of the used slot holding k, or -1 if absent.
fn find_slot[K, V](m: Map[K, V], k: K) -> int {
    let mut i = slot((m.hash)(k), m.cap);
    let mut steps = 0;
    let mut res = 0 - 1;
    let mut done = false;
    while !done && steps < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 0 { done := true; }
        else {
            if e.state == 1 && (m.eq)(e.key, k) { res := i; done := true; }
            else { i := slot(i + 1, m.cap); steps := steps + 1; }
        }
    }
    res
}

fn grow[K, V](m: Map[K, V], r: Region) -> Map[K, V] {
    let mut nm = new(r, m.cap * 2, m.ze.key, m.ze.val, m.hash, m.eq);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { nm := insert(nm, r, e.key, e.val); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    nm
}

fn insert[K, V](m0: Map[K, V], r: Region, k: K, v: V) -> Map[K, V] {
    let m = if (m0.occ + 1) * 4 >= m0.cap * 3 { grow(m0, r) } else { m0 };
    let mut i = slot((m.hash)(k), m.cap);
    let mut first_tomb = 0 - 1;
    let mut placed = false;
    let mut newcount = m.count;
    let mut newocc = m.occ;
    while !placed {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 0 {
            let dst = if first_tomb >= 0 { first_tomb } else { i };
            if dst == i { newocc := m.occ + 1; }     // consumed a fresh empty
            m.slots[dst] := Entry { state: 1, key: k, val: v };
            newcount := m.count + 1;
            placed := true;
        } else {
            if e.state == 2 {
                if first_tomb < 0 { first_tomb := i; }
                i := slot(i + 1, m.cap);
            } else {
                if (m.eq)(e.key, k) {
                    m.slots[i] := Entry { state: 1, key: k, val: v };   // overwrite
                    placed := true;
                } else {
                    i := slot(i + 1, m.cap);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Map { ..m, count: newcount, occ: newocc }
}

fn get[K, V](m: Map[K, V], k: K) -> Option[V] {
    let i = find_slot(m, k);
    if i < 0 { None } else { Some(m.slots[i].val) }
}

fn get_or[K, V](m: Map[K, V], k: K, fallback: V) -> V {
    match get(m, k) { Some(v) => v, None => fallback }
}

fn contains[K, V](m: Map[K, V], k: K) -> bool { find_slot(m, k) >= 0 }

fn remove[K, V](m: Map[K, V], k: K) -> Map[K, V] {
    let i = find_slot(m, k);
    if i < 0 {
        m
    } else {
        m.slots[i] := Entry { state: 2, key: m.ze.key, val: m.ze.val };  // tombstone
        Map { ..m, count: m.count - 1 }
    }
}

fn size[K, V](m: Map[K, V]) -> int { m.count }
fn is_empty[K, V](m: Map[K, V]) -> bool { m.count == 0 }

fn clear[K, V](m: Map[K, V]) -> Map[K, V] {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap { m.slots[i] := m.ze; i := i + 1; }
    Map { ..m, count: 0, occ: 0 }
}

fn each[K, V](m: Map[K, V], f: fn(K, V) -> ()) -> () {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { f(e.key, e.val); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
}

fn fold[A, K, V](m: Map[K, V], init: A, step: fn(A, K, V) -> A) -> A {
    let mut acc = init;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { acc := step(acc, e.key, e.val); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    acc
}

fn keys[K, V](m: Map[K, V], r: Region, zk: K) -> Vec[K] {
    let mut out = vec::new(r, m.count + 1, zk);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { out := vec::push(out, r, e.key); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    out
}

fn values[K, V](m: Map[K, V], r: Region, zv: V) -> Vec[V] {
    let mut out = vec::new(r, m.count + 1, zv);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < m.cap {
        let e = m.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { out := vec::push(out, r, e.val); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    out
}

===== END FILE: std/map.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/math.orto =====
// std/math.orto — integer and pure-float math. No allocation, no FFI.
//
// Two families, named apart (orto has no overloading, by design): the
// plain names work on `int`, the `f`-prefixed ones on `float`. Only
// functions implementable without libm live here; transcendentals
// (sin/cos/exp/log) belong to a future float module over the FFI
// escape hatch, because they genuinely need libm — not laziness.

// ---------- integer ----------

fn min(a: int, b: int) -> int { if a < b { a } else { b } }
fn max(a: int, b: int) -> int { if a > b { a } else { b } }
fn abs(a: int) -> int { if a < 0 { 0 - a } else { a } }
fn sign(a: int) -> int { if a < 0 { 0 - 1 } else { if a > 0 { 1 } else { 0 } } }

fn clamp(x: int, lo: int, hi: int) -> int {
    if x < lo { lo } else { if x > hi { hi } else { x } }
}

fn pow(base: int, exp: int) -> int {
    let mut acc = 1;
    let mut b = base;
    let mut e = exp;
    while e > 0 {
        if (e & 1) == 1 { acc := acc * b; }    // exponentiation by squaring
        b := b * b;
        e := e >> 1;
    }
    acc
}

fn gcd(a: int, b: int) -> int {
    let mut x = abs(a);
    let mut y = abs(b);
    while y != 0 {
        let t = y;
        y := x % y;
        x := t;
    }
    x
}

fn lcm(a: int, b: int) -> int {
    if a == 0 { 0 } else { if b == 0 { 0 } else { abs(a) / gcd(a, b) * abs(b) } }
}

// floor(sqrt(n)) for n >= 0, exact (integer Newton's method).
fn isqrt(n: int) -> int {
    if n < 2 {
        if n < 0 { 0 } else { n }
    } else {
        let mut x = n;
        let mut y = (x + 1) / 2;
        while y < x {
            x := y;
            y := (x + n / x) / 2;
        }
        x
    }
}

// floor(log2(n)) for n >= 1; 0 for n <= 1.
fn ilog2(n: int) -> int {
    let mut x = n;
    let mut r = 0;
    while x > 1 { x := x >> 1; r := r + 1; }
    r
}

fn is_pow2(n: int) -> bool { n > 0 && (n & (n - 1)) == 0 }

// Smallest power of two >= n (>= 1 always).
fn next_pow2(n: int) -> int {
    let mut p = 1;
    while p < n { p := p << 1; }
    p
}

fn factorial(n: int) -> int {
    let mut acc = 1;
    let mut i = 2;
    while i <= n { acc := acc * i; i := i + 1; }
    acc
}

// ---------- float ----------

fn fabs(a: float) -> float { if a < 0.0 { 0.0 - a } else { a } }
fn fmin(a: float, b: float) -> float { if a < b { a } else { b } }
fn fmax(a: float, b: float) -> float { if a > b { a } else { b } }

// Truncate toward zero.
fn ftrunc(x: float) -> float { to_float(to_int(x)) }

fn ffloor(x: float) -> float {
    let t = ftrunc(x);
    if x < t { t - 1.0 } else { t }
}

fn fceil(x: float) -> float {
    let t = ftrunc(x);
    if x > t { t + 1.0 } else { t }
}

// Round half away from zero.
fn fround(x: float) -> float {
    if x < 0.0 { fceil(x - 0.5) } else { ffloor(x + 0.5) }
}

// sqrt via Newton-Raphson; 0 for x <= 0. Converges to double precision.
fn fsqrt(x: float) -> float {
    if x <= 0.0 {
        0.0
    } else {
        let mut g = x;
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < 60 {
            g := (g + x / g) * 0.5;
            i := i + 1;
        }
        g
    }
}

===== END FILE: std/math.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/raw.orto =====
// std::raw — the unsafe namespace. Byte codecs and bit ops over raw
// `*byte` memory (foreign buffers from FFI, mmap'd files, packets).
//
// These are byte-assembly: they read/write one byte at a time at an
// explicit offset, so they are host-endian-INDEPENDENT and have no
// alignment requirement — the correct way to handle wire formats.
// The mirror of std::bin, but for `*byte` instead of Handle[byte].
//
// No bounds checks: the raw world is opt-in, the caller knows the layout.

// ---------- 16-bit ----------

fn read_u16_le(p: *byte, off: int) -> int {
    to_int(p[off]) + to_int(p[off + 1]) * 256
}

fn read_u16_be(p: *byte, off: int) -> int {
    to_int(p[off]) * 256 + to_int(p[off + 1])
}

fn write_u16_le(p: *byte, off: int, v: int) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte(v % 256);
    p[off + 1] := to_byte((v / 256) % 256);
    0
}

fn write_u16_be(p: *byte, off: int, v: int) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte((v / 256) % 256);
    p[off + 1] := to_byte(v % 256);
    0
}

// ---------- 32-bit ----------

fn read_u32_le(p: *byte, off: int) -> int {
    to_int(p[off])
    + to_int(p[off + 1]) * 256
    + to_int(p[off + 2]) * 65536
    + to_int(p[off + 3]) * 16777216
}

fn read_u32_be(p: *byte, off: int) -> int {
    to_int(p[off]) * 16777216
    + to_int(p[off + 1]) * 65536
    + to_int(p[off + 2]) * 256
    + to_int(p[off + 3])
}

fn write_u32_le(p: *byte, off: int, v: int) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte(v % 256);
    p[off + 1] := to_byte((v / 256) % 256);
    p[off + 2] := to_byte((v / 65536) % 256);
    p[off + 3] := to_byte((v / 16777216) % 256);
    0
}

fn write_u32_be(p: *byte, off: int, v: int) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte((v / 16777216) % 256);
    p[off + 1] := to_byte((v / 65536) % 256);
    p[off + 2] := to_byte((v / 256) % 256);
    p[off + 3] := to_byte(v % 256);
    0
}

// ---------- 64-bit ----------
//
// u64 returns `u64`, NOT `int`: a full u64 (bit 63 set) does not fit in
// signed i64, and assembling it with signed +/* would overflow (UB). So
// we read/write into the `u64` type and use shifts/masks (unsigned wrap
// is defined). u16/u32 stay `int` only because they fit losslessly.

fn read_u64_le(p: *byte, off: int) -> u64 {
    to_u64(to_int(p[off]))
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 1])) << 8)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 2])) << 16)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 3])) << 24)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 4])) << 32)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 5])) << 40)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 6])) << 48)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 7])) << 56)
}

fn read_u64_be(p: *byte, off: int) -> u64 {
    (to_u64(to_int(p[off])) << 56)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 1])) << 48)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 2])) << 40)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 3])) << 32)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 4])) << 24)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 5])) << 16)
    | (to_u64(to_int(p[off + 6])) << 8)
    | to_u64(to_int(p[off + 7]))
}

fn write_u64_le(p: *byte, off: int, v: u64) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte(to_int(v & 255));
    p[off + 1] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 8) & 255));
    p[off + 2] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 16) & 255));
    p[off + 3] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 24) & 255));
    p[off + 4] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 32) & 255));
    p[off + 5] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 40) & 255));
    p[off + 6] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 48) & 255));
    p[off + 7] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 56) & 255));
    0
}

fn write_u64_be(p: *byte, off: int, v: u64) -> int {
    p[off]     := to_byte(to_int((v >> 56) & 255));
    p[off + 1] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 48) & 255));
    p[off + 2] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 40) & 255));
    p[off + 3] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 32) & 255));
    p[off + 4] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 24) & 255));
    p[off + 5] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 16) & 255));
    p[off + 6] := to_byte(to_int((v >> 8) & 255));
    p[off + 7] := to_byte(to_int(v & 255));
    0
}

// ---------- byte swap (reverse byte order of a value) ----------
// 16/32 fit in int; 64 must use u64 (full range, unsigned wrap).

fn bswap16(x: int) -> int {
    (x % 256) * 256 + (x / 256) % 256
}

fn bswap32(x: int) -> int {
    (x % 256) * 16777216
    + ((x / 256) % 256) * 65536
    + ((x / 65536) % 256) * 256
    + (x / 16777216) % 256
}

fn bswap64(x: u64) -> u64 {
    ((x & 255) << 56)
    | (((x >> 8) & 255) << 48)
    | (((x >> 16) & 255) << 40)
    | (((x >> 24) & 255) << 32)
    | (((x >> 32) & 255) << 24)
    | (((x >> 40) & 255) << 16)
    | (((x >> 48) & 255) << 8)
    | ((x >> 56) & 255)
}

===== END FILE: std/raw.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/ring.orto =====
// std/ring.orto — a fixed-capacity ring (circular) buffer over a Ref.
// FIFO: push appends at the tail; when full, push drops the oldest. pop
// removes from the head. Both return the updated ring (it is a value).

struct Ring[T] { data: Handle[T], cap: int, head: int, count: int, zero: T }

fn new[T](r: Region, cap: int, zero: T) -> Ring[T] {
    let c = if cap < 1 { 1 } else { cap };
    Ring { data: ref(r, c, zero), cap: c, head: 0, count: 0, zero: zero }
}

fn size[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> int { rg.count }
fn capacity[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> int { rg.cap }
fn is_empty[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> bool { rg.count == 0 }
fn is_full[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> bool { rg.count == rg.cap }

fn push[T](rg: Ring[T], x: T) -> Ring[T] {
    let tail = (rg.head + rg.count) % rg.cap;
    rg.data[tail] := x;
    if rg.count < rg.cap {
        Ring { ..rg, count: rg.count + 1 }
    } else {
        Ring { ..rg, head: (rg.head + 1) % rg.cap }   // full -> overwrite oldest
    }
}

fn pop[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> (Ring[T], Option[T]) {
    if rg.count == 0 {
        (rg, None)
    } else {
        let x = rg.data[rg.head];
        (Ring { ..rg, head: (rg.head + 1) % rg.cap, count: rg.count - 1 }, Some(x))
    }
}

// Front element without removing it.
fn peek[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> Option[T] {
    if rg.count == 0 { None } else { Some(rg.data[rg.head]) }
}

fn clear[T](rg: Ring[T]) -> Ring[T] { Ring { ..rg, head: 0, count: 0 } }

// Visit elements oldest-first.
fn each[T](rg: Ring[T], f: fn(T) -> ()) -> () {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < rg.count {
        f(rg.data[(rg.head + i) % rg.cap]);
        i := i + 1;
    }
}

===== END FILE: std/ring.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/set.orto =====
// std/set.orto — a set is a map with no values: Map[T, ()]. Thin layer
// over `map`; hash/eq are injected exactly the same way and stored in the
// underlying map. Set operations (union/intersect/difference) build a
// fresh set carrying the same hash/eq as the left operand.

use map::{Map};
use vec::{Vec};

fn make[T](r: Region, cap: int, zt: T,
           hash: fn(T) -> int, eq: fn(T, T) -> bool) -> Map[T, ()] {
    map::new(r, cap, zt, (), hash, eq)
}

fn add[T](s: Map[T, ()], r: Region, x: T) -> Map[T, ()] { map::insert(s, r, x, ()) }
fn has[T](s: Map[T, ()], x: T) -> bool { map::contains(s, x) }
fn remove[T](s: Map[T, ()], x: T) -> Map[T, ()] { map::remove(s, x) }
fn size[T](s: Map[T, ()]) -> int { map::size(s) }
fn is_empty[T](s: Map[T, ()]) -> bool { map::is_empty(s) }

fn each[T](s: Map[T, ()], f: fn(T) -> ()) -> () {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < s.cap {
        let e = s.slots[i];
        if e.state == 1 { f(e.key); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
}

// All elements of a, then all of b.
fn union[T](r: Region, a: Map[T, ()], b: Map[T, ()]) -> Map[T, ()] {
    let zt = a.ze.key;
    let mut out = map::new(r, a.cap + b.cap, zt, (), a.hash, a.eq);
    let ka = map::keys(a, r, zt);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < vec::size(ka) { out := map::insert(out, r, vec::get(ka, i), ()); i := i + 1; }
    let kb = map::keys(b, r, zt);
    i := 0;
    while i < vec::size(kb) { out := map::insert(out, r, vec::get(kb, i), ()); i := i + 1; }
    out
}

// Elements present in both a and b.
fn intersect[T](r: Region, a: Map[T, ()], b: Map[T, ()]) -> Map[T, ()] {
    let zt = a.ze.key;
    let mut out = map::new(r, a.cap, zt, (), a.hash, a.eq);
    let ka = map::keys(a, r, zt);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < vec::size(ka) {
        let x = vec::get(ka, i);
        if map::contains(b, x) { out := map::insert(out, r, x, ()); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    out
}

// True iff every element of a is also in b.
fn is_subset[T](r: Region, a: Map[T, ()], b: Map[T, ()]) -> bool {
    let ka = map::keys(a, r, a.ze.key);
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut ok = true;
    while i < vec::size(ka) {
        if !map::contains(b, vec::get(ka, i)) { ok := false; i := vec::size(ka); }
        else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    ok
}

// Elements in a but not in b.
fn difference[T](r: Region, a: Map[T, ()], b: Map[T, ()]) -> Map[T, ()] {
    let zt = a.ze.key;
    let mut out = map::new(r, a.cap, zt, (), a.hash, a.eq);
    let ka = map::keys(a, r, zt);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < vec::size(ka) {
        let x = vec::get(ka, i);
        if !map::contains(b, x) { out := map::insert(out, r, x, ()); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    out
}

===== END FILE: std/set.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/sort.orto =====
// std/sort.orto — in-place sort of a Handle[T] (or slice) with an explicit
// comparator. cmp(a, b) < 0 means a comes before b, 0 equal, > 0 after.
// The comparator is injected — no ordering is baked into any type
// (use str::compare for strings, a field-projecting closure for records).
//
// Quicksort with a median-of-three pivot (so already-sorted and
// reverse-sorted inputs don't degrade to O(n^2)), falling back to
// insertion sort on small ranges where it's faster.

fn swap[T](xs: Handle[T], i: int, j: int) -> () {
    let t = xs[i];
    xs[i] := xs[j];
    xs[j] := t;
}

fn insertion[T](xs: Handle[T], lo: int, hi: int, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> () {
    let mut i = lo + 1;
    while i <= hi {
        let key = xs[i];
        let mut j = i - 1;
        while j >= lo && cmp(xs[j], key) > 0 {
            xs[j + 1] := xs[j];
            j := j - 1;
        }
        xs[j + 1] := key;
        i := i + 1;
    }
}

// Index of the median of xs[a], xs[b], xs[c].
fn med3[T](xs: Handle[T], a: int, b: int, c: int, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> int {
    if cmp(xs[a], xs[b]) < 0 {
        if cmp(xs[b], xs[c]) < 0 { b }
        else { if cmp(xs[a], xs[c]) < 0 { c } else { a } }
    } else {
        if cmp(xs[a], xs[c]) < 0 { a }
        else { if cmp(xs[b], xs[c]) < 0 { c } else { b } }
    }
}

// Lomuto partition around the pivot already parked at `hi`.
fn partition[T](xs: Handle[T], lo: int, hi: int, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> int {
    let pivot = xs[hi];
    let mut i = lo - 1;
    let mut j = lo;
    while j < hi {
        if cmp(xs[j], pivot) <= 0 { i := i + 1; swap(xs, i, j); }
        j := j + 1;
    }
    swap(xs, i + 1, hi);
    i + 1
}

fn qsort[T](xs: Handle[T], lo: int, hi: int, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> () {
    if hi - lo < 12 {
        insertion(xs, lo, hi, cmp);
    } else {
        let mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
        let p = med3(xs, lo, mid, hi, cmp);
        swap(xs, p, hi);                       // park median as pivot at hi
        let q = partition(xs, lo, hi, cmp);
        qsort(xs, lo, q - 1, cmp);
        qsort(xs, q + 1, hi, cmp);
    }
}

fn sort[T](xs: Handle[T], cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> () {
    if len(xs) > 1 { qsort(xs, 0, len(xs) - 1, cmp); }
}

fn is_sorted[T](xs: Handle[T], cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> bool {
    let mut i = 1;
    let mut ok = true;
    while i < len(xs) {
        if cmp(xs[i - 1], xs[i]) > 0 { ok := false; i := len(xs); }
        else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    ok
}

// Binary search in a sorted Ref. Returns an index of a matching element,
// or -1 if absent. cmp is the same ordering used to sort.
fn bsearch[T](xs: Handle[T], key: T, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> int {
    let mut lo = 0;
    let mut hi = len(xs) - 1;
    let mut found = 0 - 1;
    while lo <= hi {
        let mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
        let c = cmp(xs[mid], key);
        if c == 0 { found := mid; lo := hi + 1; }
        else { if c < 0 { lo := mid + 1; } else { hi := mid - 1; } }
    }
    found
}

// First index i with cmp(xs[i], key) >= 0 (insertion point keeping order).
fn lower_bound[T](xs: Handle[T], key: T, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> int {
    let mut lo = 0;
    let mut hi = len(xs);
    while lo < hi {
        let mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
        if cmp(xs[mid], key) < 0 { lo := mid + 1; } else { hi := mid; }
    }
    lo
}

// First index i with cmp(xs[i], key) > 0.
fn upper_bound[T](xs: Handle[T], key: T, cmp: fn(T, T) -> int) -> int {
    let mut lo = 0;
    let mut hi = len(xs);
    while lo < hi {
        let mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2;
        if cmp(xs[mid], key) <= 0 { lo := mid + 1; } else { hi := mid; }
    }
    lo
}

===== END FILE: std/sort.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/str.orto =====
// std/str.orto — operations over Handle[byte], the one string type. A string
// is just a byte buffer living in a region; these are ordinary functions
// that take the region only when they allocate. Trimming and slicing
// return views (no copy); building a new string copies into the region.
//
// `hash`/`compare` make Handle[byte] usable as a map key and as a sort key
// (inject str::hash + str::eq into map::new, str::compare into sort::sort).

use vec::{Vec, new, push, slice_of};

// ---------- predicates / inspection ----------

fn is_empty(s: Handle[byte]) -> bool { len(s) == 0 }

fn eq(a: Handle[byte], b: Handle[byte]) -> bool {
    if len(a) != len(b) {
        false
    } else {
        let mut i = 0;
        let mut same = true;
        while i < len(a) {
            if to_int(a[i]) != to_int(b[i]) { same := false; i := len(a); }
            else { i := i + 1; }
        }
        same
    }
}

// Lexicographic order: <0 if a<b, 0 if equal, >0 if a>b.
fn compare(a: Handle[byte], b: Handle[byte]) -> int {
    let n = if len(a) < len(b) { len(a) } else { len(b) };
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut r = 0;
    while i < n {
        let ca = to_int(a[i]);
        let cb = to_int(b[i]);
        if ca != cb { r := if ca < cb { 0 - 1 } else { 1 }; i := n; }
        else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    if r != 0 { r } else { len(a) - len(b) }
}

// FNV-1a (32-bit constants, accumulated in a 64-bit int — wraps, fine
// for hashing). Pair with str::eq when using strings as map keys.
fn hash(s: Handle[byte]) -> int {
    let mut h = 2166136261;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < len(s) {
        h := (h ^ to_int(s[i])) * 16777619;
        i := i + 1;
    }
    h
}

// True iff `s[pos..pos+len(pat)]` equals `pat`. The shared kernel for
// find / contains / starts_with / ends_with / count / replace.
fn match_at(s: Handle[byte], pos: int, pat: Handle[byte]) -> bool {
    let m = len(pat);
    if pos < 0 || pos + m > len(s) {
        false
    } else {
        let mut j = 0;
        let mut ok = true;
        while j < m {
            if to_int(s[pos + j]) != to_int(pat[j]) { ok := false; j := m; }
            else { j := j + 1; }
        }
        ok
    }
}

// Index of first occurrence of byte c, or -1.
fn index_of(s: Handle[byte], c: int) -> int {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut found = 0 - 1;
    while i < len(s) {
        if to_int(s[i]) == c { found := i; i := len(s); }
        else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    found
}

// Index of last occurrence of byte c, or -1.
fn last_index_of(s: Handle[byte], c: int) -> int {
    let mut i = len(s) - 1;
    let mut found = 0 - 1;
    while i >= 0 {
        if to_int(s[i]) == c { found := i; i := 0 - 1; }
        else { i := i - 1; }
    }
    found
}

// Index of first occurrence of substring `needle`, or -1. Empty matches 0.
fn find(s: Handle[byte], needle: Handle[byte]) -> int {
    let m = len(needle);
    if m == 0 {
        0
    } else {
        let mut i = 0;
        let mut res = 0 - 1;
        while i + m <= len(s) {
            if match_at(s, i, needle) { res := i; i := len(s) + 1; }
            else { i := i + 1; }
        }
        res
    }
}

fn contains(s: Handle[byte], needle: Handle[byte]) -> bool { find(s, needle) >= 0 }
fn starts_with(s: Handle[byte], pre: Handle[byte]) -> bool { match_at(s, 0, pre) }
fn ends_with(s: Handle[byte], suf: Handle[byte]) -> bool {
    match_at(s, len(s) - len(suf), suf)
}

// Count of non-overlapping occurrences of `needle`.
fn count(s: Handle[byte], needle: Handle[byte]) -> int {
    let m = len(needle);
    if m == 0 {
        0
    } else {
        let mut i = 0;
        let mut n = 0;
        while i + m <= len(s) {
            if match_at(s, i, needle) { n := n + 1; i := i + m; }
            else { i := i + 1; }
        }
        n
    }
}

// ---------- views (no allocation) ----------

fn sub(s: Handle[byte], lo: int, hi: int) -> Handle[byte] { slice(s, lo, hi) }

fn is_ws(c: int) -> bool { c == 32 || c == 9 || c == 10 || c == 13 }

fn trim_left(s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < len(s) && is_ws(to_int(s[i])) { i := i + 1; }
    slice(s, i, len(s))
}

fn trim_right(s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let mut j = len(s);
    while j > 0 && is_ws(to_int(s[j - 1])) { j := j - 1; }
    slice(s, 0, j)
}

fn trim(s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] { trim_left(trim_right(s)) }

// ---------- allocation ----------

fn dup(r: Region, s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let n = len(s);
    let out = ref(r, n + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < n { out[i] := s[i]; i := i + 1; }
    slice(out, 0, n)
}

fn concat(r: Region, a: Handle[byte], b: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let la = len(a);
    let lb = len(b);
    let out = ref(r, la + lb + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < la { out[i] := a[i]; i := i + 1; }
    let mut j = 0;
    while j < lb { out[la + j] := b[j]; j := j + 1; }
    slice(out, 0, la + lb)
}

fn repeat(r: Region, s: Handle[byte], n: int) -> Handle[byte] {
    let m = len(s);
    let total = if n < 0 { 0 } else { m * n };
    let out = ref(r, total + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut k = 0;
    let mut pos = 0;
    while k < n {
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < m { out[pos] := s[i]; pos := pos + 1; i := i + 1; }
        k := k + 1;
    }
    slice(out, 0, total)
}

fn reverse(r: Region, s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let n = len(s);
    let out = ref(r, n + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < n { out[i] := s[n - 1 - i]; i := i + 1; }
    slice(out, 0, n)
}

fn to_upper(r: Region, s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let n = len(s);
    let out = ref(r, n + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < n {
        let c = to_int(s[i]);
        out[i] := if c >= 97 && c <= 122 { to_byte(c - 32) } else { s[i] };
        i := i + 1;
    }
    slice(out, 0, n)
}

fn to_lower(r: Region, s: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let n = len(s);
    let out = ref(r, n + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < n {
        let c = to_int(s[i]);
        out[i] := if c >= 65 && c <= 90 { to_byte(c + 32) } else { s[i] };
        i := i + 1;
    }
    slice(out, 0, n)
}

// Render an int to its decimal byte string.
fn from_int(r: Region, n: int) -> Handle[byte] {
    if n == 0 {
        dup(r, "0")
    } else {
        let neg = n < 0;
        let mut m = if neg { 0 - n } else { n };
        let mut ndig = 0;
        let mut t = m;
        while t > 0 { ndig := ndig + 1; t := t / 10; }
        let total = if neg { ndig + 1 } else { ndig };
        let buf = ref(r, total + 1, to_byte(0));
        let mut i = total - 1;
        while m > 0 {
            buf[i] := to_byte(48 + m % 10);
            m := m / 10;
            i := i - 1;
        }
        if neg { buf[0] := to_byte(45); }
        slice(buf, 0, total)
    }
}

// Parse a decimal int with optional leading '-'. None on empty/garbage.
fn parse_int(s: Handle[byte]) -> Option[int] {
    let n = len(s);
    if n == 0 {
        None
    } else {
        let neg = to_int(s[0]) == 45;
        let start = if neg || to_int(s[0]) == 43 { 1 } else { 0 };
        if start >= n {
            None
        } else {
            let mut acc = 0;
            let mut i = start;
            let mut ok = true;
            while i < n {
                let c = to_int(s[i]);
                if c < 48 || c > 57 { ok := false; i := n; }
                else { acc := acc * 10 + (c - 48); i := i + 1; }
            }
            if ok { Some(if neg { 0 - acc } else { acc }) } else { None }
        }
    }
}

// ---------- composing many pieces ----------

fn join(r: Region, parts: Handle[Handle[byte]], sep: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let np = len(parts);
    let mut total = 0;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < np { total := total + len(parts[i]); i := i + 1; }
    if np > 0 { total := total + len(sep) * (np - 1); }
    let out = ref(r, total + 1, to_byte(0));
    let mut pos = 0;
    i := 0;
    while i < np {
        if i > 0 {
            let mut k = 0;
            while k < len(sep) { out[pos] := sep[k]; pos := pos + 1; k := k + 1; }
        }
        let p = parts[i];
        let mut j = 0;
        while j < len(p) { out[pos] := p[j]; pos := pos + 1; j := j + 1; }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    slice(out, 0, total)
}

// Split on a separator byte into a Vec of views into `s` (no copy).
fn split(r: Region, s: Handle[byte], sep: int) -> Vec[Handle[byte]] {
    let empty = slice(s, 0, 0);
    let mut v = new(r, 4, empty);
    let mut start = 0;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < len(s) {
        if to_int(s[i]) == sep {
            v := push(v, r, slice(s, start, i));
            start := i + 1;
        }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    push(v, r, slice(s, start, len(s)))
}

// Replace every non-overlapping occurrence of `old` with `neww`.
fn replace(r: Region, s: Handle[byte], old: Handle[byte], neww: Handle[byte]) -> Handle[byte] {
    let m = len(old);
    if m == 0 {
        dup(r, s)
    } else {
        let mut v = new(r, len(s) + 4, to_byte(0));
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < len(s) {
            if match_at(s, i, old) {
                let mut j = 0;
                while j < len(neww) { v := push(v, r, neww[j]); j := j + 1; }
                i := i + m;
            } else {
                v := push(v, r, s[i]);
                i := i + 1;
            }
        }
        slice_of(v)
    }
}

===== END FILE: std/str.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/time.orto =====
// std/time.orto — monotonic clock, modelled on Go's time package.
//
// `now()` returns a monotonic instant in nanoseconds (int is 64-bit, so
// it never wraps in practice). Durations are plain int nanoseconds; the
// unit helpers below name them like Go (time.Second, time.Millisecond).
//
//     let t0 = time::now();
//     ... work ...
//     let elapsed_ms = time::since(t0) / time::millisecond();

extern fn orto_now_ns() -> i64
extern fn orto_now_us() -> i32
extern fn orto_now_ms() -> i32

// Monotonic instant, nanoseconds.
fn now() -> int { to_int(orto_now_ns()) }

// Nanoseconds elapsed since an instant captured with now().
fn since(start: int) -> int { now() - start }

// Coarser clocks (microseconds / milliseconds), if ns is overkill.
fn now_us() -> int { to_int(orto_now_us()) }
fn now_ms() -> int { to_int(orto_now_ms()) }

// Duration units, in nanoseconds — name a duration like Go does.
fn nanosecond()  -> int { 1 }
fn microsecond() -> int { 1000 }
fn millisecond() -> int { 1000000 }
fn second()      -> int { 1000000000 }

===== END FILE: std/time.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: std/vec.orto =====
// std/vec.orto — a growable buffer backed by a Ref in a region.
//
// Vec is a value (a small handle struct). Allocating operations take the
// region explicitly and RETURN the updated Vec — like Go's append:
//     v = vec::push(v, r, x);
// `zero` is a sample value of T used to fill freshly grown backing cells
// (a generic T has no implicit default, so the caller provides one once).
//
// Behaviour over T is injected per call (contains/index_of take `eq`,
// map/filter/find take a closure) — never bound to the type.

struct Vec[T] { data: Handle[T], count: int, cap: int, zero: T }

fn new[T](r: Region, cap: int, zero: T) -> Vec[T] {
    let c = if cap < 1 { 1 } else { cap };
    Vec { data: ref(r, c, zero), count: 0, cap: c, zero: zero }
}

// Build a Vec by copying an existing Ref (slice) of length n.
fn from_ref[T](r: Region, src: Handle[T], zero: T) -> Vec[T] {
    let n = len(src);
    let c = if n < 1 { 1 } else { n };
    let d = ref(r, c, zero);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < n { d[i] := src[i]; i := i + 1; }
    Vec { data: d, count: n, cap: c, zero: zero }
}

// ---------- size ----------

fn size[T](v: Vec[T]) -> int { v.count }
fn capacity[T](v: Vec[T]) -> int { v.cap }
fn is_empty[T](v: Vec[T]) -> bool { v.count == 0 }

// ---------- element access ----------

fn get[T](v: Vec[T], i: int) -> T { v.data[i] }
fn set[T](v: Vec[T], i: int, x: T) -> () { v.data[i] := x; }
fn first[T](v: Vec[T]) -> Option[T] { if v.count == 0 { None } else { Some(v.data[0]) } }
fn last[T](v: Vec[T]) -> Option[T] {
    if v.count == 0 { None } else { Some(v.data[v.count - 1]) }
}

// A view over the live elements (a slice of the backing). Cheap, shares memory.
fn slice_of[T](v: Vec[T]) -> Handle[T] { slice(v.data, 0, v.count) }

// ---------- growth / shrink (return a new handle) ----------

fn push[T](v: Vec[T], r: Region, x: T) -> Vec[T] {
    if v.count < v.cap {
        v.data[v.count] := x;
        Vec { ..v, count: v.count + 1 }
    } else {
        let newcap = v.cap * 2;
        let nd = ref(r, newcap, v.zero);
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < v.count { nd[i] := v.data[i]; i := i + 1; }
        nd[v.count] := x;
        Vec { data: nd, count: v.count + 1, cap: newcap, zero: v.zero }
    }
}

fn pop[T](v: Vec[T]) -> (Vec[T], Option[T]) {
    if v.count == 0 {
        (v, None)
    } else {
        let x = v.data[v.count - 1];
        (Vec { ..v, count: v.count - 1 }, Some(x))
    }
}

fn clear[T](v: Vec[T]) -> Vec[T] { Vec { ..v, count: 0 } }

fn truncate[T](v: Vec[T], n: int) -> Vec[T] {
    if n < v.count { Vec { ..v, count: if n < 0 { 0 } else { n } } } else { v }
}

fn extend[T](v: Vec[T], r: Region, other: Vec[T]) -> Vec[T] {
    let mut acc = v;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < other.count { acc := push(acc, r, other.data[i]); i := i + 1; }
    acc
}

fn insert_at[T](v: Vec[T], r: Region, idx: int, x: T) -> Vec[T] {
    if idx >= v.count {
        push(v, r, x)
    } else {
        let nv = push(v, r, x);              // x lands at the end, count grows
        let mut j = nv.count - 1;
        while j > idx { swap(nv, j, j - 1); j := j - 1; }
        nv
    }
}

fn remove_at[T](v: Vec[T], idx: int) -> Vec[T] {
    if idx < 0 || idx >= v.count {
        v
    } else {
        let mut i = idx;
        while i < v.count - 1 { v.data[i] := v.data[i + 1]; i := i + 1; }
        Vec { ..v, count: v.count - 1 }
    }
}

fn copy[T](v: Vec[T], r: Region) -> Vec[T] { from_ref(r, slice_of(v), v.zero) }

// ---------- in-place reordering ----------

fn swap[T](v: Vec[T], i: int, j: int) -> () {
    let t = v.data[i];
    v.data[i] := v.data[j];
    v.data[j] := t;
}

fn reverse[T](v: Vec[T]) -> () {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut j = v.count - 1;
    while i < j { swap(v, i, j); i := i + 1; j := j - 1; }
}

// ---------- iteration / transformation ----------

fn each[T](v: Vec[T], f: fn(T) -> ()) -> () {
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < v.count { f(v.data[i]); i := i + 1; }
}

fn fold[A, T](v: Vec[T], init: A, step: fn(A, T) -> A) -> A {
    let mut acc = init;
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < v.count { acc := step(acc, v.data[i]); i := i + 1; }
    acc
}

fn map[T, U](v: Vec[T], r: Region, f: fn(T) -> U, zu: U) -> Vec[U] {
    let mut out = new(r, v.count + 1, zu);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < v.count { out := push(out, r, f(v.data[i])); i := i + 1; }
    out
}

fn filter[T](v: Vec[T], r: Region, pred: fn(T) -> bool) -> Vec[T] {
    let mut out = new(r, v.count + 1, v.zero);
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < v.count {
        if pred(v.data[i]) { out := push(out, r, v.data[i]); }
        i := i + 1;
    }
    out
}

fn find[T](v: Vec[T], pred: fn(T) -> bool) -> Option[T] {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut res: Option[T] = None;
    while i < v.count {
        if pred(v.data[i]) { res := Some(v.data[i]); i := v.count; }
        else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    res
}

fn any[T](v: Vec[T], pred: fn(T) -> bool) -> bool {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut r = false;
    while i < v.count {
        if pred(v.data[i]) { r := true; i := v.count; } else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    r
}

fn all[T](v: Vec[T], pred: fn(T) -> bool) -> bool {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut r = true;
    while i < v.count {
        if pred(v.data[i]) { i := i + 1; } else { r := false; i := v.count; }
    }
    r
}

// eq injected — orto has no per-type equality.
fn index_of[T](v: Vec[T], x: T, eq: fn(T, T) -> bool) -> int {
    let mut i = 0;
    let mut found = 0 - 1;
    while i < v.count {
        if eq(v.data[i], x) { found := i; i := v.count; } else { i := i + 1; }
    }
    found
}

fn contains[T](v: Vec[T], x: T, eq: fn(T, T) -> bool) -> bool {
    index_of(v, x, eq) >= 0
}

===== END FILE: std/vec.orto =====

===== BEGIN FILE: runtime_qbe.c =====
/* QBE-backend runtime. Linked with the qbe-generated assembly.
   Mirrors orto's region model: a slab per Region, generation-checked
   Handles (use-after-free/reset aborts). Region = {long slot; long gen};
   Handle = {long slot; long off; long len; long gen}. All passed by
   pointer from the generated code (no aggregate ABI). */
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

typedef struct { char *buf; long cap; long used; long gen; int live; } RtReg;
#define ORTO_MAXR 1048576
static RtReg RT[ORTO_MAXR];
static long  rt_top = 0;

static long reg_alloc(long n) {
  long s = rt_top++;
  if (s >= ORTO_MAXR) abort();
  RT[s].buf = (n > 0) ? (char *)malloc((size_t)n) : (char *)malloc(1);
  if (!RT[s].buf) abort();
  RT[s].cap = n; RT[s].used = 0; RT[s].gen += 1; RT[s].live = 1;
  return s;
}

/* out = {slot, gen} */
void orto_rt_region(long n, long *out) {
  long s = reg_alloc(n);
  out[0] = s; out[1] = RT[s].gen;
}

static void chk_reg(long *r) { if (!RT[r[0]].live || RT[r[0]].gen != r[1]) abort(); }
static void chk_h(long *h)   { if (!RT[h[0]].live || RT[h[0]].gen != h[3]) abort(); }

/* allocate `count` cells of `elemsize` bytes in region r, optionally
   filling each with the `elemsize` bytes at init (NULL = leave zeroed).
   out = Handle{slot, off, len, gen}. */
void orto_rt_ref(long *r, long count, long elemsize, void *init, long *out) {
  chk_reg(r);
  long s = r[0];
  long off = RT[s].used;
  long bytes = count * elemsize;
  if (off + bytes > RT[s].cap) abort();
  RT[s].used += bytes;
  if (init) { for (long i = 0; i < count; i++) memcpy(RT[s].buf + off + i*elemsize, init, (size_t)elemsize); }
  else      { memset(RT[s].buf + off, 0, (size_t)bytes); }
  out[0] = s; out[1] = off; out[2] = count; out[3] = r[1];
}

/* address of element i, gen+bounds checked (abort on failure). */
void *orto_rt_at(long *h, long i, long elemsize) {
  chk_h(h);
  if (i < 0 || i >= h[2]) abort();
  return RT[h[0]].buf + h[1] + i * elemsize;
}

/* address of element i, or NULL if stale/out-of-bounds (no abort) — for try_at. */
void *orto_rt_try(long *h, long i, long elemsize) {
  if (!RT[h[0]].live || RT[h[0]].gen != h[3]) return 0;
  if (i < 0 || i >= h[2]) return 0;
  return RT[h[0]].buf + h[1] + i * elemsize;
}

long orto_rt_len(long *h) { chk_h(h); return h[2]; }

/* base pointer of a handle's bytes (as_ptr / array_data) — gen checked. */
void *orto_rt_data(long *h) { chk_h(h); return RT[h[0]].buf + h[1]; }

void orto_rt_reset(long *r) {
  long s = r[0];
  RT[s].gen += 1; RT[s].used = 0; r[1] = RT[s].gen;   /* refresh the binding */
}

void orto_rt_drop(long *r) {
  long s = r[0];
  if (RT[s].live) { free(RT[s].buf); RT[s].buf = 0; RT[s].live = 0; RT[s].gen += 1; }
}

void orto_rt_slice(long *h, long lo, long hi, long elemsize, long *out) {
  chk_h(h);
  if (lo < 0 || hi > h[2] || lo > hi) abort();
  out[0] = h[0]; out[1] = h[1] + lo * elemsize; out[2] = hi - lo; out[3] = h[3];
}

/* wrap static/foreign bytes as a permanently-live Handle (string literals). */
void orto_rt_wrap(void *data, long len, long *out) {
  long s = rt_top++;
  if (s >= ORTO_MAXR) abort();
  RT[s].buf = (char *)data; RT[s].cap = len; RT[s].used = len;
  RT[s].gen += 1; RT[s].live = 1;
  out[0] = s; out[1] = 0; out[2] = len; out[3] = RT[s].gen;
}

/* string-literal equality for `match s { "lit" => ... }`. h = Handle to
   the scrutinee bytes; data/len = the literal. */
long orto_rt_streq(long *h, void *data, long len) {
  if (RT[h[0]].gen != h[3] || !RT[h[0]].live) return 0;
  if (h[2] != len) return 0;
  return memcmp(RT[h[0]].buf + h[1], data, (size_t)len) == 0 ? 1 : 0;
}

===== END FILE: runtime_qbe.c =====
